# The Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Regulations 2005

> **Warning: changes not yet applied to this text (36).** Text as at 2005-12-22. See https://www.legislation.gov.uk/changes/affected/ssi/2005/599
>
> Extent: Scotland.

*Extent: S*

2005 No. 599

AGRICULTURE

PESTICIDES

The Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Regulations 2005

Made

28th November 2005

Laid before the Scottish Parliament

30th November 2005

Coming into force

22nd December 2005

The Scottish Ministers, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 2(2) of the European Communities Act 1972[^f00001] and of all other powers enabling them in that behalf, hereby make the following Regulations:

## 1. Citation, commencement and extent[^key-c5d730162f2179d0ab0a6297e0095492]

(1) These Regulations may be cited as the Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Regulations 2005 and shall come into force on 22nd December 2005.

(2) These Regulations shall extend to Scotland only.

## 2. Interpretation[^key-5afb9025df4874bb6f9d3172272ec3d0]

(1) In these Regulations–

- “Directive 76/895” means Council Directive [76/895/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1976/0895) relating to the fixing of maximum levels for pesticide residues in and on fruit and vegetables[^f00002];
- “Directive 86/362” means Council Directive [86/362/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1986/0362) on the fixing of maximum levels for pesticide residues in and on cereals[^f00003];
- “Directive 86/363” means Council Directive [86/363/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1986/0363) on the fixing of maximum levels for pesticide residues in and on foodstuffs of animal origin[^f00004];
- “Directive 90/642” means Council Directive [90/642/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1990/0642) on the fixing of maximum levels for pesticide residues in and on certain products of plant origin, including fruit and vegetables[^f00005];
- “EEA State” means a Member State, Norway, Iceland or Liechtenstein;
- “fruit or vegetable” means a product referred to in Article 1 of Directive 90/642;
- “maximum residue level” means the maximum quantity of pesticide residue (measured in milligrams per kilogram of product) that a product is permitted to contain under regulation 3;
- “pesticide” means any substance, preparation or organism listed in column 1 of Schedule 1;
- “pesticide residue” means, in relation to any particular pesticide, the substance specified in column 2 of Schedule 1 in respect of that pesticide;
- “product” means any crop, food or feeding stuff specified in Schedules 2 or 3;
- “putting into circulation”, in relation to any product, means handing it over (post harvest if the product is a fruit or vegetable), whether or not for consideration and any related expression shall be construed accordingly; and
- “the Residues Directives” means Directive 76/895, Directive 86/362, Directive 86/363 and Directive 90/642, in each case as amended on the date these Regulations are made.

(2) The words and expressions “composite food”, “drying” and “processing” when used in regulation 3 or in paragraph (c) or (d) of regulation 5 have the same meaning as when used in the Residues Directives and any related expressions shall be construed accordingly.

(3) Any reference in these Regulations to a numbered Schedule or regulation shall be construed as a reference to the Schedule or, as the case may be, regulation so numbered in these Regulations.

(4) Any reference in any Schedule to these Regulations to any product, figure or pesticide includes any qualifying words relating to that product, figure or pesticide in that Schedule.

## 3. Prohibition on putting into circulation products with excess residues[^key-2e08c657c25b8f2bde08fe5d1a2c4797]

(1) No person shall put into circulation a product named in Schedule 2 if it contains a quantity of pesticide residue, per kilogram of the product, greater than that specified in that Schedule in respect of that product and the pesticide in question, during the period (if any) specified.

(2) Subject to paragraph (3), paragraph (1) shall also apply in relation to the putting into circulation of–

- (a) any product which after drying or processing is obtained from any of the products named in Schedule 2, and
- (b) any composite food which includes any of the products named in that Schedule,

as it applies to the products so named.

(3) Where–

- (a) paragraph (1) applies in relation to a dried or processed product or a composite food by virtue of paragraph (2), and
- (b) in relation to that dried or processed product or composite food no quantity has been specified in Schedule 2 as the maximum residue level in respect of a pesticide residue which may be contained in that dried or processed product or composite food,

paragraph (1) applies as if the maximum residue level in respect of that pesticide residue were the quantity applicable under that Schedule as it has effect by virtue of regulation 5(c) or, as the case may be, (d).

(4) Any person who, without reasonable excuse, contravenes or causes or permits any other person to contravene the prohibition in paragraph (1) shall be guilty of an offence, and shall be liable–

- (a) on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum; and
- (b) on conviction on indictment, to a fine.

(5) In any proceedings for an offence under this regulation in relation to any product, whether or not dried or processed or a composite food, it is a defence for the person charged to prove that when the product was put into circulation–

- (a) it was so put with the intention of its being exported to a country which is not an EEA State and the contravention of the prohibition in paragraph (1) was caused by the product being treated in a manner–
  - (i) required by the country of destination in order to prevent the introduction of harmful organisms into its territory; or
  - (ii) necessary to protect the product from harmful organisms during transport to the country of destination and storage there, or
- (b) it was so put with the intention that–
  - (i) it be used in the manufacture of things other than foodstuffs and animal feed; or
  - (ii) it be used for sowing or planting.

(6) Sections 19 (enforcement powers), 21(5) (offences – penalties etc) and 22 (general defence of due diligence) of, and Schedule 2 (officers and their powers) to, the Food and Environment Protection Act 1985[^f00006] shall apply for the purposes of this regulation as they apply for the purposes of that Act, taking references in those sections to that Act or any Part of it to be references to this regulation, and the general purposes of that Act to include the purposes of this regulation.

## 4. Seizure or disposal of crops, food or feeding stuffs[^key-e06d1e59d4597940a9f6cb5bf1aa0c42]

(1) If any product contains a quantity of pesticide residue greater than that permitted under regulation 3(1), the Scottish Ministers may–

- (a) seize or dispose of the consignment containing that product, or any part of it, or require the owner or any person appearing to be in charge of it to dispose of it, or
- (b) direct the owner or any person appearing to be in charge of it to take such remedial action as appears to the Scottish Ministers to be necessary.

(2) Paragraph (1) applies to any product put into circulation in circumstances referred to in regulation 3(5)(a) or (b) as it applies to other products.

## 5. Sampling and analysis[^key-317d20a2cb20509ab5fecec0717339b1]

In determining for the purposes of regulation 3(1) whether the quantity of pesticide residue contained in any product exceeds the maximum residue level–

- (a) in relation to any product specified in column 2 of Schedule 3 (and falling within a group of products specified in column 1 of that Schedule) the whole or part only of that product shall, so far as is practicable, be taken into account as specified in column 3 of that Schedule;
- (b) the procedure to be followed for sampling for the determination of pesticide residues shall be that set out in the Annex to Commission Directive [2002/63/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2002/0063)[^f00007];
- (c) in the case of any product which has been dried or processed, Schedule 2 shall have effect where, in relation to a pesticide residue, no such maximum residue level is specified therein for the product in its dried or processed form, as if the maximum residue level specified in that Schedule in respect of that pesticide residue and in relation to the product in question were subject to an adjustment to take account of the concentration of the product caused by the drying process or, as the case may be, the dilution or concentration of the product caused by the processing; and
- (d) in a case where a product listed in Schedule 2 has been mixed with other products or ingredients to form a composite food, that Schedule shall have effect, in relation to that composite food, as if the products which have been mixed to form the composite food had not been mixed and accordingly the maximum residue levels specified for each of the pesticide residues specified applied in relation to each of those products separately taking into account–
  - (i) the relative concentrations of each of the products in the composite food; and
  - (ii) the provisions of paragraph (c).

## 6. Revocations[^key-2b0d0ae3c61e46d482b56056c8997255]

The Regulations specified in Schedule 4 are revoked.

ROSS FINNIE

A member of the Scottish Executive

St Andrew’s House, Edinburgh

28th November 2005

<tfoot>
<tr><td colspan="206">UNITS:<br>Maximum residue levels (MRLs) are expressed in milligrammes of residue per kilogramme of food.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">KEY:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">*<br>Level at or about the limit of determination.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">FOOTNOTES:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(1)<br>Paddy or rough rice, husked rice and semi-milled or wholly milled rice.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(2)<br>Levels are measured on fat, except in the case of foods with a fat content of 10% or less by weight. In these cases the residue is related to the total weight of the boned foodstuff and the MRL is one tenth of the value given in the table, but must be no less than 0.01 mg/kg.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(3)<br>These levels are for fresh raw cow’s milk and fresh whole cream cow’s milk expressed on the whole milk.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(4)<br>For preserved, concentrated or sweetened cow’s milk; for raw milk and whole cream milk of another animal origin; and for butter, cheese or curd. Whether made from cow’s milk or other milk of a combination, the following levels apply:<br>if the fat content is less than 2% by weight, the MRL is taken as half that set for raw milk and whole cream milk;<br>if the fat content is 2% or more by weight, the MRL is expressed in mg/kg of fat and is set at 25 times that set for raw milk and whole cream milk.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(5)<br>Birds' eggs in shell (other than eggs for hatching) and whole egg products and egg yolk products (whether fresh, dried or otherwise prepared).</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(6)<br>Scarole includes broad-leaf endive.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(7)<br>For eggs and egg products with a fat content higher than 10%, the maximum level is expressed in mg/kg fat. In this case, the maximum level is 10 times higher than the maximum level for fresh eggs.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(8)<br>Kidney except of poultry.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(9)<br>All other meat, edible offal, fat and preparations of meat and edible offal.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(10)<br>All meat.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(11)<br>All liver and kidney.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(12)<br>Liver of bovine animals.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(13)<br>Broccoli includes calabrese.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(14)<br>Meat of poultry.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(15)<br>Meat of bovine animals.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(16)<br>Fat of bovine animals.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(17)<br>Except poultry.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(18)<br>Liver of chicken.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(19)<br>Kidney of bovine animals.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(20)<br>Liver of bovine animals, sheep and goats.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(21)<br>Except foodstuffs of ovine origin.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(22)<br>Meat of bovine animals, sheep and goats.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(23)<br>Except meet and liver of bovine animals, sheep and poultry or meat of poultry.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(24)<br>This MRL also applies to spelt.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(25)<br>Except spelt.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(26)<br>Liver of bovine animals, sheep, goats, and swine.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(27)<br>Kidney of bovine animals, sheep, goats, and swine.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(28)<br>Meat of poultry, fat and edible offal.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(29)<br>Meat of bovine animals, sheep, goats, and swine.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(30)<br>All kidney.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(31)<br>Kidney of swine</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(32)<br>Kidney of bovine animals sheep and goats.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(33)<br>This figure is the sum of the alpha and beta isomers.<br>For meat, fat &amp; preparations of meat MRL for aplha isomer is 0.2 mg/kg and MRL for beta isomer is 0.1 mg/kg.<br>For milk and dairy produce MRL for alpha isomer is 0.004 mg/kg and MRL for beta isomer is 0.003 mg/kg.<br>For eggs MRL for alpha isomer is 0.02 mg/kg and MRL for beta isomer is 0.01 mg/kg.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(34)<br>All meat, liver and fat.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(358)<br>1 mg/kg applies to whole seeds; 0.05 mg/kg applies to seed without shell.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(36)<br>Ruminant liver.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(37)<br>Fat liver and kidney.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="206">(38)<br>With the exception of meat and other ovine, bovine and caprine products.</td></tr>

## SCHEDULE 3 — Regulations 2(1) and 5(a)[^key-bd756371f70f13a540d2bf3f64479d81]

<table>
<thead>
<tr><td>Column 1</td><td>Column 2</td><td>Column 3</td></tr>
<tr><th><em>Group of products</em></th><th><em>Products included in the groups</em></th><th><em>Part of product to which maximum residue levels apply</em></th></tr>
</thead>
<tfoot>
<tr><td colspan="3"><em>Note:</em> The word “fresh” extends to products which have been chilled</td></tr>
</tfoot>
<tbody>
<tr><td colspan="3">1. Fruit, fresh, dried or uncooked, preserved by freezing, not containing added sugar; nuts</td></tr>
<tr><td>(i) CITRUS FRUIT</td><td>Grapefruit<br>Lemons<br>Limes<br>Mandarins (including clementines and similar hybrids)<br>Oranges<br>Pomelos<br>Others</td><td>Whole Product</td></tr>
<tr><td>(ii) TREE NUTS (shelled or unshelled)</td><td>Almonds<br>Brazil nuts<br>Cashew nuts<br>Chestnuts<br>Coconuts<br>Hazelnuts<br>Macadamia nuts<br>Pecans<br>Pine nuts<br>Pistachios<br>Walnuts<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of shell</td></tr>
<tr><td>(iii) POME FRUIT</td><td>Apples<br>Pears<br>Quinces<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of stems</td></tr>
<tr><td>(iv) STONE FRUIT</td><td>Apricots<br>Cherries<br>Peaches (including nectarines and similar hybrids)<br>Plums<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of stems</td></tr>
<tr><td>(v) BERRIES AND SMALL FRUIT</td><td>(a) <em>Table and wine grapes</em><br>Table grapes<br>Wine grapes<br>(b) <em>Strawberries</em> (other than wild)<br>(c) <em>Cane fruit</em> (other than wild)<br>Blackberries<br>Dewberries<br>Loganberries<br>Raspberries<br>Others<br>(d) <em>Other small fruit and berries</em> (other than wild)<br>Bilberries<br>Cranberries<br>Currants (red, black and white)<br>Gooseberries<br>Others<br>(e) <em>Wild berries and wild fruit</em></td><td>Whole product after removal of caps and stems (if any) and, in the case of currants, fruits with stems</td></tr>
<tr><td>(vi) MISCELLANEOUS</td><td>Avocados<br>Bananas<br>Dates<br>Figs<br>Kiwi fruit<br>Kumquats<br>Litchis<br>Mangoes<br>Olives (table consumption)†<br>Olives (oil extract)<br>Papaya<br>Passion fruit<br>Pineapples<br>Pomegranates<br>Others</td><td>Whole fruit after removal of stems (if any) and in the case of pineapple, after removal of the crown</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>† Whole fruit after removal of stems (if any), after removal of soil (if any) by rinsing in running water</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3">2. Vegetables, fresh or uncooked, frozen or dry</td></tr>
<tr><td>(i) ROOT AND TUBER VEGETABLES</td><td>Beetroot<br>Carrots<br>Celeriac<br>Horseradish<br>Jerusalem artichokes<br>Parsnips<br>Parsley root<br>Radishes<br>Salsify<br>Sweet potatoes<br>Swedes<br>Turnips<br>Yams<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of tops and adhering soil (if any) (removal of soil by rinsing in running water or by gentle brushing of the dry product)</td></tr>
<tr><td>(ii) BULB VEGETABLES</td><td>Garlic<br>Onions<br>Shallots<br>Spring onions<br>Others</td><td>For dry onions, shallots and garlic: whole product after removal of easily detachable skin and soil (if any); onions, shallots and garlic other than dry, spring onions: whole product after removal of roots and soil (if any)</td></tr>
<tr><td>(iii) FRUITING VEGETABLES</td><td>(a) <em>Solanacea</em><br>Tomatoes<br>Peppers<br>Chilli peppers<br>Aubergines<br>Others<br>(b) <em>Cucurbits—edible peel</em><br>Cucumbers<br>Gherkin<br>Courgettes<br>Others<br>(c) <em>Cucurbits—inedible peel</em><br>Melons<br>Squashes<br>Watermelons<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of stems</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>(d) <em>Sweet corn</em></td><td>Kernels or cobs without husks</td></tr>
<tr><td>(iv) BRASSICA VEGETABLES</td><td>(a) <em>Flowering brassicas</em><br>Broccoli<br>Cauliflower<br>Others</td><td>Cauliflower and broccoli curd only</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>(b) <em>Head brassicas</em><br>Brussels sprouts<br>Head cabbage<br>Others<br>(c) <em>Leafy brassicas</em><br>Chinese cabbage<br>Kale<br>Others</td><td>Product after removal of decayed leaves (if any)</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>(d) <em>Kohlrabi</em></td><td>Whole product after removal of tops and adhering soil (if any) (removal of soil by rinsing in running water or by gentle brushing of the dry product)</td></tr>
<tr><td>(v) LEAF VEGETABLES AND FRESH HERBS</td><td>(a) <em>Lettuce and similar</em><br>Cress<br>Lamb’s lettuce<br>Lettuce<br>Scarole<br>Others<br>(b) <em>Spinach and similar</em><br>Spinach<br>Beet leaves (chard)<br>Others<br>(c) <em>Watercress</em><br>(d) <em>Witloof</em><br>(e) <em>Herbs</em><br>Chervil<br>Chives<br>Parsley<br>Celery Leaves<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of decayed outer leaves, root and soil (if any)</td></tr>
<tr><td>(vi) LEGUME VEGETABLES (FRESH)</td><td>Beans (with pods)<br>Beans (without pods)<br>Peas (with pods)<br>Peas (without pods)<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of pods or with pods if they are intended to be eaten</td></tr>
<tr><td>(vii) STEM VEGETABLES</td><td>Asparagus<br>Cardoons<br>Celery<br>Fennel<br>Globe artichokes<br>Leeks<br>Rhubarb<br>Others</td><td>Whole product after removal of decayed tissue and soil (if any); leeks and fennel: whole product after removal of roots and soil (if any)</td></tr>
<tr><td>(viii) FUNGI</td><td>Mushrooms (other than wild)<br>Wild Mushrooms</td><td>Whole product after removal of soil or growing medium</td></tr>
<tr><td>3. Pulses</td><td>Beans<br>Lentils<br>Peas<br>Others</td><td>Whole product</td></tr>
<tr><td>4. Oil seeds</td><td>Linseed<br>Peanuts<br>Poppy seed<br>Rape seed<br>Sesame seed<br>Sunflower seed<br>Soya bean<br>Others</td><td>Whole seed or kernel after removal of shell and husk when possible</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>Whole seed, including shell when present, and whole seed without shell, when the shell is absent</td></tr>
<tr><td>5. Potatoes</td></tr>
<tr><td>Early potatoes<br>Ware potatoes</td><td>Whole product after removal of soil (if any) (removal of soil by rinsing in running water or by gentle brushing of the dry product)</td></tr>
<tr><td>6. Tea</td><td>(dried leaves and stalks, fermented or otherwise, Camellia sinensis)</td><td>Whole product</td></tr>
<tr><td>7. Hops</td><td>(dried), including hop pellets and unconcentrated powder</td><td>Whole product</td></tr>
<tr><td>8. Cereals</td><td>Wheat<br>Rye<br>Barley<br>Sorghum<br>Oats<br>Triticale<br>Maize<br>Buckwheat<br>Millet<br>Rice<br>Other cereals</td><td>Whole grain without husk</td></tr>
<tr><td>9. Foodstuffs of animal origin</td><td>Meat, fat and preparations of meat</td><td>Whole commodity (for fat soluble pesticides a portion of carcase fat is analysed and maximum residue levels apply to carcase fat)</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>Milk</td><td>Whole commodity</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>Eggs</td><td>Whole egg whites and yolks combined after removal of shells</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

| The Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Amendment (No. 2) Regulations 2004 | [S.S.I. 2004/220](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2004/220) |
| The Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Amendment (No. 3) Regulations 2004 | [S.S.I. 2004/399](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2004/399) |
| The Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Amendment Regulations 2005 | [S.S.I. 2005/109](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2005/109) |
| The Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Amendment (No. 2) Regulations 2005 | [S.S.I. 2005/281](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2005/281) |

(This note is not part of the Regulations)

These Regulations, which extend to Scotland only, are made under section 2(2) of the European Communities Act 1972 and consolidate and replace the provisions of the Pesticides (Maximum Residue Levels in Crops, Food and Feeding Stuffs) (Scotland) Regulations 2000 ([S.S.I. 2000/22](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2000/22)) as amended by [S.S.I. 2001/84](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2001/84), [S.S.I. 2001/221](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2001/221), [S.S.I. 2001/435](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2001/435), [S.S.I. 2002/271](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2002/271), [S.S.I. 2002/489](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2002/489), [S.S.I. 2003/118](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2003/118), [S.S.I. 2003/445](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2003/445), [S.S.I. 2004/104](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2004/104), [S.S.I. 2004/220](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2004/220), [S.S.I. 2004/399](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2004/399), [S.S.I. 2005/109](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2005/109) and [S.S.I. 2005/281](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ssi/2005/281) – see regulation 6 and Schedule 4 for revocations.

The Regulations specify maximum levels of pesticide residues which crops, food and feeding stuffs may contain in implementation of: Council Directive [76/895/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1976/0895) (O.J. No. L 340, 9.12.1976, p.26) relating to fruit and vegetables; Council Directive [86/362/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1986/0362) (OJ No. L 221, 7.8.86, p.37) and Council Directive [86/363/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1986/0363) (O.J. No. L 221, 7.8.86, p.43) as regards cereals and products of animal origin; and Council Directive [90/642/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1990/0642) (O.J. No. L 350, 14.12.90, p.71) as regards certain products of plant origin (including fruit and vegetables), as amended (these Directives as so amended being referred to in these Regulations as “the Residues Directives”).

In particular, these Regulations specify new maximum residue levels on products of plant origin including cereals for the pesticides Carfentrazone-ethyl, Fenamidone, Isoxaflutole, Maleic Hydrazide, Mecoprop, Propyzamide, Trifloxystrobin in implementation of Commission Directive [2005/37/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2005/0037) (O.J. No. L 141, 4.6.2005, p.10); on products of plant origin, cereals and foodstuffs of animal origin for Amitraz in implementation of Commission Directive [2005/46/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2005/0046) (O.J. No. L 177, 9.7.2005, p.35); and on products of plant origin, cereals and foodstuffs of animal origin for Flufenacet, Fosthiazate, Iodosulfuron-methyl sodium, Iprodione, Mesotrione, Molinate, Picoxystrobin, Propiconazole, Silthiofam in implementation of Commission Directive [2005/48/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2005/0048) (O.J. No. L 219, 24.8.2005, p.29).

Regulation 3 also creates offences, specifies penalties, provides defences and confers enforcement powers where maximum residue levels have been exceeded in respect of products put into circulation.

The Regulations also confer powers to seize and dispose of products where maximum residue levels have been exceeded (regulation 4) and prescribe how much of a particular product is to be taken into account in determining whether a maximum residue level has been exceeded in accordance with Council Directive [90/642/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1990/0642) (regulation 5(a) and Schedule 3). Provision is also made with regard to the manner for determining whether maximum residue levels have been exceeded when found in dried or processed products or composite foods, so far as these are the subject of the Residues Directives (regulation 5(c) and (d)).

A regulatory impact assessment and transposition note have been prepared in respect of these Regulations and placed in the Scottish Parliament Information Centre. Copies of the assessment and note can be obtained from the Scottish Executive Environment and Rural Affairs Department, EPHAS2, Area 1-B, Pentland House, 47 Robb’s Loan, Edinburgh, EH14 1TY.

[^f01033]: Footnote: (33)

    This figure is the sum of the alpha and beta isomers.

    For meat, fat & preparations of meat MRL for aplha isomer is 0.2 mg/kg and MRL for beta isomer is 0.1 mg/kg.

    For milk and dairy produce MRL for alpha isomer is 0.004 mg/kg and MRL for beta isomer is 0.003 mg/kg.

    For eggs MRL for alpha isomer is 0.02 mg/kg and MRL for beta isomer is 0.01 mg/kg.

[^f01003]: Footnote: (3)

    These levels are for fresh raw cow’s milk and fresh whole cream cow’s milk expressed on the whole milk.

[^f01004]: Footnote: (4)

    For preserved, concentrated or sweetened cow’s milk; for raw milk and whole cream milk of another animal origin; and for butter, cheese or curd. Whether made from cow’s milk or other milk of a combination, the following levels apply:

    - if the fat content is less than 2% by weight, the MRL is taken as half that set for raw milk and whole cream milk;

    - if the fat content is 2% or more by weight, the MRL is expressed in mg/kg of fat and is set at 25 times that set for raw milk and whole cream milk.

[^f01005]: Footnote: (5)

    Birds' eggs in shell (other than eggs for hatching) and whole egg products and egg yolk products (whether fresh, dried or otherwise prepared).

[^f01007]: Footnote: (7)

    For eggs and egg products with a fat content higher than 10%, the maximum level is expressed in mg/kg fat. In this case, the maximum level is 10 times higher than the maximum level for fresh eggs.
